# SUMMARY

1. Project Title/Subtitle

Study on the Analysis and Derivation of Factors Influencing Groundwater Usage Estimation (1st Year)

2. Research Period

202426 > 20241231

3. Obj ective of Research

This project is part of the "Groundwater Usage Estimation Methodology Advancement Initiative" Its purpose is to validate, analyze, and supplement the currently collected groundwater usage monitoring data and influencing factors Additionally, it aims to identify and implement necessary measures to support the timely revision of the usage estimation formula, scheduled for establishment in 2026, thereby enhancing its reliability

To achieve this, the project involves analyzing the currently collected groundwater usage monitoring data to identify and apply improvements to the monitoring process It also assesses the reliability and utility of data to be used in the future development of groundwater usage estimation methods Ultimately, the project seeks to ensure the accuracy of national groundwater usage statistics, thereby enhancing the credibility of water resource policies

4. Content, Scope and Method of Research

(1) Current status of groundwater usage statistics survey

- Groundwater act and groundwater usage survey
- Comprehensive groundwater usage survey (200632012)
- Current groundwater usage estimation models
- Issues with existing groundwater usage estimation methods

(2) Domestic and international groundwater usage estimation methods

- Domestic groundwater usage estimation methods

- International groundwater usage estimation methods

(3) Analysis of groundwater usage monitoring data characteristics

- Establishment of a groundwater usage monitoring data database

- Analysis of characteristics of groundwater usage monitoring points by purpose

- Comparison of groundwater usage by purpose and characteristics of monitoring points

- Daily and monthly usage characteristics analysis

- Analysis of outlier characteristics for groundwater usage by monitoring point

- Outlier analysis of groundwater usage time series data

- Techniques for handling missing groundwater usage data

(4) Directions for improving groundwater usage monitoring

- Comparative analysis with past groundwater usage surveys

- Comparative analysis with usage charge data

- Evaluation of sample adequacy and improvement suggestions for groundwater usage monitoring

- Improvement measures for groundwater usage monitoring

(5) Development plan for groundwater usage prediction models

- Improvement strategies for groundwater usage estimation models

(6) Policy improvement strategies for groundwater usage

- Policy/institutional proposals for enhancing groundwater usage statistics

## 5. Research Result

(1) Issues with current groundwater usage estimation methods

- Limitations of the current estimation methods: The currently utilized groundwater usage estimation technique is based on a first-order linear regression model, which does not adequately capture the characteristics of groundwater usage While the current method includes slight variations in variables depending on the classification of areas (urban, mixed urban-rural, rural) and rainfall characteristics (below-average, average, above-average rainfall regions), the criteria for these classifications and rainfall characteristics are not clearly defined Furthermore, the criteria are based on conditions at the time of their establishment, failing to account for temporal changes in regional characteristics

- Applicability issues: The linear regression-based estimation technique cannot be applied to estimate groundwater usage at the level of individual wells It is designed for estimating the total groundwater usage at broader administrative levels, such as counties or cities (10,000 to 30,000 wells) Consequently, using this model to estimate groundwater usage in smaller areas, such as towns or villages, can result in significant errors

(2) Domestic and international groundwater usage estimation methods

- Domestic methods: Includes estimation guidelines from the 2016 Groundwater Usage Estimation Manual, measured usage methods in Jeju, usage estimation via regression tree models, and agricultural water demand forecasting techniques

- International methods: Direct estimation methods using extraction measurements, pump speed, and pumping duration, and indirect methods such as crop water demand, satellite and GIS-based estimation, groundwater modeling, WTF (Water Table Fluctuation), and artificial intelligence-based approaches

(3) Analysis of groundwater usage monitoring data characteristics

- Database construction: Collected usage monitoring data from 6,295 sites, processed missing values, and established a database

- Statistical analysis: Conducted basic statistical analyses (eg, daily/monthly usage, standard deviation, minimum, maximum, quartiles) and examined groundwater usage characteristics on daily and monthly scales

- Correlation analysis: Analyzed relationships between specific-purpose groundwater usage and monitoring site characteristics, considering facility specifications, topography, geology, soil, and nearby rivers

- Outlier analysis: Identified outliers in daily/monthly usage using box plots and analyzed temporal outliers using Shewhart control charts

(4) Directions for improving groundwater usage monitoring

- Comparative analysis: Compared past comprehensive survey data to analyze changes in sample size, usage, and standard deviation

- Sample adequacy evaluation: Assessed sample adequacy in ongoing monitoring projects and proposed strategies for purposes where additional samples are required or expanded sampling is ineffective

- Improvement measures: Recommended clarifying the distinction between monitoring and sample surveys, transitioning to sample-based surveys, establishing sample selection criteria, and aligning with groundwater-related laws and policies

(5) Development plan for groundwater usage prediction models

- Model development requirements: Addressed issues with current estimation methods, emphasizing the need for accurate daily usage estimation goals, the importance of influencing factors, and the inclusion of input variables for the models

- Reviewed prediction model types using input-output variables and developed preliminary models for certain specific purposes to compare their characteristics

- Data integration: Highlighted the importance of integrating real-world usage data and various continuous and categorical data characteristics into the model

(6) Policy improvement strategies for groundwater usage

- Legal and institutional reforms: Proposed enhancements to regulations under the Groundwater Act, institutionalization of sample-based surveys, and periodic revisions to usage estimation models

- Automated estimation systems: Suggested integrating estimation models into the Saeol Administrative System for automated usage calculations and reporting results to the Ministry of Environment (National Groundwater Information Center)

- New usage reporting systems: Advocated for mandatory reporting of actual usage data for newly developed groundwater wells to improve estimation models and statistical data

- System enhancements: Recommended creating independent systems for each region, real-time synchronization of the National Groundwater Information Center with the Saeol Administrative System, and adding functions for managing groundwater usage fees Called for a consistent database to support integrated quantity and quality management of groundwater
